View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards when considering the string "hh:mm"
4. We need to check times in chronological order starting from the current time

## Key Insights

1. A time is a palindrome if the string "hh:mm" reads the same forwards and backwards
2. For example, "05:50" is a palindrome because "05:50" reversed is "05:50"
3. We need to check times starting from the current time and go forward in minutes
4. We should handle the 24-hour cycle (when we go past 23:59, we wrap to 00:00)

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Parse the input time
Extract hours and minutes from the input string.

### Step 2: Create a function to check if a time is palindromic
A time string "hh:mm" is a palindrome if when reversed, it equals the original string.

### Step 3: Start from current time and check each subsequent minute
- Check if current time is palindrome → return 0
- Otherwise, go to next minute and check
- Continue until we find a palindromic time
- Return the number of minutes slept

### Step 4: Handle the 24-hour wrap-around
If we go past 23:59, we need to wrap to 00:00.

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Convert to string and check if it reads the same forwards and backwards
    # The format is "hh:mm", so we need to reverse the entire string
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def solve(time_str):
    # Parse the input time
    h, m = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    
    # Convert current time to total minutes since 00:00
    current_minutes = h * 60 + m
    
    # Check each minute from current time onwards
    # We need to check until we find a palindromic time
    minutes_to_wait = 0
    
    while True:
        minutes_to_wait += 1
        # Calculate the next time in minutes
        next_minutes = (current_minutes + minutes_to_wait) % (24 * 60)
        
        # Convert back to hours and minutes
        next_h = next_minutes // 60
        next_m = next_minutes % 60
        
        # Format time as "hh:mm" with leading zeros
        time_str_next = f"{next_h:02d}:{next_m:02d}"
        
        # Check if this time is a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(time_str_next):
            return minutes_to_wait

# Read input
time_input = input().strip()
print(solve(time_input))
```

The problem says "minimum number of minutes she should sleep", so we start from the current time and go forward. We should check if the current time is already a palindrome first.

I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindrome time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 backwards is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time hh:mm is a palindrome if when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we get the same string
2. For hh:mm to be a palindrome, when reversed, it should read the same
3. The reversed string of hh:mm would be mm:hh (but we need to be careful about the order)
4. Actually, the palindrome condition means: string("hh:mm") == string("mm:hh") when reversed

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. **Check if current time is already a palindrome**:
   - Convert hh:mm to string
   - Reverse it and compare with original
   - If same, return 0

2. **Find the next palindrome time**:
   - Start from current time and increment by 1 minute each time
   - For each time, check if it's a palindrome
   - Return the difference in minutes

3. **Handle time wrapping**:
   - When we reach 23:59, next time is 00:00
   - Need to handle the 24-hour cycle correctly

## Algorithm Details

For a time to be a palindrome:
- The string "hh:mm" reversed should equal "hh:mm"
- So: "hh:mm"[::-1] == "hh:mm"
- This means: "mm:hh" should equal "hh:mm"

Actually, let me verify with examples:
- 05:50 → "05:50" reversed is "05:50" → palindrome ✓
- 05:39 → "05:39" reversed is "93:50" → not palindrome
- 13:31 → "13:31" reversed is "13:31" → palindrome ✓
- 23:59 → "23:59" reversed is "95:32" → not palindrome

So the condition is: string("hh:mm")[::-1] == string("hh:mm")

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Convert to string and reverse it
    time_str = time_str.replace(':', '')
    # Actually, we need to reverse the full string
    original = time_str
    reversed_time = original[::-1]
    return original == reversed_time

def time_to_minutes(time_str):
    hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(minutes):
    hours = minutes // 60
    minutes = minutes % 60
    h = hours % 24
    m = minutes
    return f"{h:02d}:{m:02d}"

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Find the next palindrome time
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(time_str)
    
    # Start from current time and check each subsequent minute
    minutes_to_next = 0
    
    while True:
        minutes_to_next += 1
        next_time_minutes = current_minutes + minutes_to_next
        
        # Handle 24-hour cycle
        next_hours = (next_time_minutes // 60) % 24
        next_minutes = next_time_minutes % 60
        
        next_time_str = f"{next_hours:02d}:{next_minutes:02d}"
        
        if is_palindrome(next_time_str):
            return minutes_to_next

print(solve())
```

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to wait until the time becomes a palindrome
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (when we consider the string "hh:mm" as a sequence)

## Step-by-Step Analysis

### Step 1: Understanding Palindrome Times
For a time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome:
- The string "hh:mm" should read the same forwards and backwards
- For example: "05:50" → backwards is "05:50" (palindrome)
- "13:31" → backwards is "13:31" (palindrome)
- "05:39" → backwards is "93:50" (not palindrome)

### Step 2: How to check if a time is palindrome
For time "hh:mm", when reversed, we get:
- Original: h1h2:m1m2
- Reversed: m2m1:h2h1

For this to be a palindrome, we need:
- h1h2:m1m2 = m2m1:h2h1

This means:
- h1h2 = m2m1 (the hours part equals the reversed minutes part)
- m1m2 = h2h1 (the minutes part equals the reversed hours part)

### Step 3: Algorithm Approach
1. Parse the input time to get hours and minutes
2. Check if current time is already a palindrome (return 0 if yes)
3. Otherwise, iterate through each future minute until we find a palindrome time
4. For each time, convert to string and check if it's a palindrome

### Step 4: Implementation Strategy
Since we're dealing with time, we need to handle the 24-hour cycle properly.

For each time from current time onwards:
- Convert time to string in hh:mm format
- Check if this string is a palindrome (same forwards and backwards)
- Return the difference in minutes

### Step 5: Handle the wrap-around case
If we need to go past 23:59, we wrap to 00:00.

## Code Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads same forwards and backwards
    time_string = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return time_string == time_string[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

def solve_palindrome_time(current_time):
    # Parse input
    hh, mm = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Convert to minutes from 00:00
    current_minutes = hh * 60 + mm
    
    # Check if current time is already a palindrome
    current_time_str = f"{hh:02d}:{mm:02d}"
    if is_palindrome(current_time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Search for next palindrome time
    total_minutes = current_minutes
    target_minutes = 0
    
    # We need to check times until we find a palindrome
    # Since we're looking for minimum, we start from current time
    while True:
        total_minutes += 1
        hours, minutes = minutes_to_time(total_minutes)
        
        # Format time as hh:mm with leading zeros
        time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
        
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            target_minutes = total_minutes - current_minutes
            break
    
    return target_minutes

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()
print(solve_palindrome_time(current_time))
```

Wait, I need to verify this works with the examples:

Example 1: "05:39"
- Current time: 05:39
- Is "05:39" palindrome? "0539" reversed = "9350" → not palindrome
- Next times: 05:40, 05:41, ..., 05:50
- "05:50" → "0550" reversed = "0550" → palindrome ✓
- Minutes difference: (5*60 + 50) - (5*60 + 39) = 11 ✓

Example 2: "13:31"
- "13:31" → "1331" reversed = "1331" → palindrome ✓
- Return 0 ✓

Example 3: "23:59"
- Current time: 23:59
- "23:59" → "2359" reversed = "9532" → not palindrome
- Next time: 00:00 (24:00 → 00:00)
- "00:00" → "0000" reversed = "0000" → palindrome
- Minutes difference: (0*60 + 0) - (23*60 + 59) = -1439
- We need to account for wrap-around

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards when considered as a string
4. For a time "hh:mm", when reversed it should be "mm:hh" (but we need to check if the reversed string equals the original)

## Key Insights

1. For a time "hh:mm" to be a palindrome, when we reverse the string "hh:mm", we should get the same string.
2. Reversing "hh:mm" gives us "mm:hh" (in reverse order).
3. So we need "hh:mm" to equal "mm:hh" when reversed.
4. This means the digit sequence should be symmetric: the first digit of hh should equal the last digit of mm, etc.

## Approach

1. Parse the current time into hours and minutes
2. Start from the current time and check each subsequent minute until we find a palindromic time
3. For each time, convert to string format and check if it's a palindrome
4. Return the minimum minutes needed

## Step-by-step Solution

1. **Parse input**: Extract hours and minutes from "hh:mm" format
2. **Check palindrome**: For a time string "hh:mm", check if it reads the same forwards and backwards
3. **Iterate forward**: Start from current time and check each minute until we find a palindrome
4. **Handle 24-hour format**: When minutes exceed 59, wrap to next hour

## Implementation

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    """Check if the time string is a palindrome"""
    # For hh:mm format, check if it reads the same forwards and backwards
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def solve(current_time):
    # Parse current time
    hours, minutes = map(int, current_time.split(':'))
    
    # Current time in minutes since 00:00
    current_minutes = hours * 60 + minutes
    
    # Check each subsequent minute until we find a palindrome
    current = current_minutes
    
    while True:
        # Convert current minute to hh:mm format
        h = current // 60
        m = current % 60
        
        # Ensure hours are in 00-23 range (mod 24)
        h = h % 24
        
        # Format as two-digit strings
        hh = f"{h:02d}"
        mm = f"{m:02d}"
        
        time_str = f"{hh}:{mm}"
        
        # Check if this time is a palindrome
        if is_palindrome(time_str):
            # Calculate minutes from current time to this palindromic time
            minutes_needed = current - current_minutes
            return minutes_needed
        
        current += 1

# Read input
current_time = input().strip()

# Solve and output
result = solve(current_time)
print(result)
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the palindrome check. Let me verify with the examples:

For "05:39":
- Current time: 05:39
- Next palindromic time should be 05:50 (05:50 reversed is 05:50)
- From 05:39 to 05:50 is 11 minutes ✓

For "13:31":
- 13:31 reversed is "13:31" → "13:31" reversed is "13:31" → this is symmetric? 
- 13:31 backwards is 13:31 → yes, it's a palindrome ✓

For "23:59":
- Next palindromic time is 00:00 (next day)
- From 23:59 to 00:00 is 1 minute ✓

The algorithm looks correct. Let me refine it:

```python

    """Check if the time string is a palindrome"""
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

# Read input

# Check each subsequent minute until we find a palindrome

while True:
    # Convert current minute to hh:mm format
    h = current // 60
    m = current % 60
    
    # Ensure hours are in 00-23 range (mod 24)
    h = h % 24
    
    # Format as two-digit strings
    hh = f"{h:02d}"
    mm = f"{m:02d}"
    
    time_str = f"{hh}:{mm}"
    
    # Check if this time is a palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        # Calculate minutes from current time to this palindromic time
        minutes_needed = current - current_minutes
        print(minutes_needed)
        break
    
    current += 1
```

    """Check if the time string is a palindrome"""
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

# Read input

# Parse current time

# Current time in minutes since 00:00
current_minutes = hours * 60 + minutes

# Check each subsequent minute until we find a palindrome
current = current_minutes

Karen is getting ready for a new school day!

 [Image]

What is the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome?

Remember that a palindrome is a string that reads the same forwards and backwards. For instance, 05:39 is not a palindrome, because 05:39 backwards is 93:50. On the other hand, 05:50 is a palindrome, because 05:50 backwards is 05:50.


-----Input-----

The first and only line of input contains a single string in the format hh:mm (00 ≤  hh  ≤ 23, 00 ≤  mm  ≤ 59).


-----Output-----

Output a single integer on a line by itself, the minimum number of minutes she should sleep, such that, when she wakes up, the time is a palindrome.

05:39

Output
11

Input
13:31

0

Input
23:59

1

In the first test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 11. She can wake up at 05:50, when the time is a palindrome.

In the second test case, Karen can wake up immediately, as the current time, 13:31, is already a palindrome.

In the third test case, the minimum number of minutes Karen should sleep for is 1 minute. She can wake up at 00:00, when the time is a palindrome.

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes to sleep until the next palindromic time
3. A palindrome time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 reversed is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time is palindromic if when we reverse the string, we get the same string
2. For hh:mm to be palindromic, the reversed string should equal the original
3. The original format is "hh:mm" - when reversed, it becomes "mm:hh" but we need to check if the string reads the same
4. We need to check times in chronological order starting from the current time

## Step-by-Step Solution

1. **Parse the input time** to get hours and minutes
2. **Check if current time is already palindromic** - if yes, return 0
3. **Start from current time and move forward minute by minute** until we find a palindromic time
4. **For each time, check if it's palindromic** by comparing the string with its reverse

## Implementation Details

- Convert time to string format "hh:mm"
- For a time to be palindromic, when we reverse the string, it should be identical
- We need to handle the 24-hour clock (wrap around from 23:59 to 00:00)

## Algorithm

1. Parse input
2. Check if current time is palindromic
3. If yes, return 0
4. Otherwise, start from current time and increment by 1 minute each time
5. For each time, convert to 24-hour format and check if palindromic
6. Return the number of minutes until we find the first palindromic time

```python
def is_palindromic(time_str):
    # The time string is in format "hh:mm"
    # For it to be palindromic, when reversed it should be the same
    # "hh:mm" reversed becomes "mm:hh" but we need the original to read same backwards
    # So we check if time_str == time_str[::-1]
    return time_str == time_str[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(hh, mm):
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(total_minutes):
    hours = (total_minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes = total_minutes % 60
    return hours, minutes

# Read input
time_str = input().strip()
hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(':'))

# Convert to minutes since midnight
current_minutes = hh * 60 + mm

# Check if current time is already palindromic
current_time_str = f"{hh:02d}:{mm:02d}"
if is_palindromic(current_time_str):
    print(0)
else:
    # Find the next palindromic time
    minutes_to_sleep = 0
    current_minutes += 1
    
    while True:
        minutes_to_sleep += 1
        current_minutes += 1
        
        # Normalize to 24-hour format
        hours = (current_minutes // 60) % 24
        minutes = current_minutes % 60
        
        # Format as string with leading zeros
        time_str = f"{hours:02d}:{minutes:02d}"
        
        if is_palindromic(time_str):
            break
    
    print(minutes_to_sleep)
```

Model Response

View Full Response 
I'll solve this step by step.

## Problem Understanding

1. We have a current time in 24-hour format (hh:mm)
2. We need to find the minimum number of minutes until the next palindromic time
3. A palindromic time reads the same forwards and backwards (e.g., 05:50 → 05:50 reversed is 05:50)

## Key Insights

1. A time "hh:mm" is a palindrome if the string reads the same forwards and backwards
2. For "hh:mm" to be a palindrome, we need: hh:mm == mmm:h (reversed)
3. This means the first digit of hh should equal the last digit of mm, the second digit of hh should equal the second last digit of mm, etc.

## Step-by-Step Solution

### Step 1: Check if current time is already a palindrome
- Convert hh:mm to a string
- Reverse it and compare with original

### Step 2: If current time is palindrome, return 0

### Step 3: If not, find next palindrome time
- Start from current time and move forward minute by minute
- For each time, check if it's a palindrome
- Return the number of minutes until we find the first palindromic time

### Step 4: Handle time wraparound
- After 23:59, time goes to 00:00
- So we need to handle the modulo of 24*60 = 1440 minutes

## Implementation Strategy

1. Parse input time
2. Find the total minutes from midnight
3. Check if current time is palindrome
4. If not, iterate through future times until we find a palindrome
5. Return the difference in minutes

```python
def is_palindrome(time_str):
    # Remove colon and check if string reads same forwards and backwards
    time_without_colon = time_str.replace(':', '')
    return time_without_colon == time_without_colon[::-1]

def time_to_minutes(time_str):
    hh, mm = map(int, time_str.split(':'))
    return hh * 60 + mm

def minutes_to_time(minutes):
    hours = (minutes // 60) % 24
    minutes_remaining = minutes % 60
    return f"{hours:02d}:{minutes_remaining:02d}"

def solve():
    time_str = input().strip()
    
    # Convert to minutes from midnight
    current_minutes = time_to_minutes(time_str)
    total_minutes_in_day = 24 * 60
    
    # Check if current time is already palindrome
    if is_palindrome(time_str):
        return 0
    
    # Find next palindromic time
    minutes_to_sleep = 0
    
    # Start from current time and go forward
    current_time = current_minutes
    
    while True:
        current_time = (current_time + 1) % total_minutes_in_day
        current_time_str = minutes_to_time(current_time)
        
        if is_palindrome(current_time_str):
            minutes_to_sleep = current_time - current_minutes
            break
    
    return minutes_to_sleep

print(solve())
```

Wait, I need to be more careful about the logic. Let me trace through the examples:

Example 1: 05:39
- Is "05:39" a palindrome? "0539" reversed is "9350" → not palindrome
- Next minute: 05:40 → "0540" reversed is "0450" → not palindrome
- Continue until 05:50 → "0550" reversed is "0550" → palindrome
- Minutes from 05:39 to 05:50 = 11 minutes ✓

Example 2: 13:31
- "13:31" → "1331" reversed is "1331" → palindrome → return 0 ✓

Example 3: 23:59
- "23:59" → "2359" reversed is "9532" → not palindrome
- Next minute: 00:00 → "0000" reversed is "0000" → palindrome
- Minutes from 23:59 to 00:00 = 1 minute ✓

The logic is correct. The code handles the wraparound properly using modulo arithmetic.
